   880→// GetLatestCommitMessageOnBranch returns the commit message of the latest commit on the given branch.
   881→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCommitMessageOnBranch(branchName string) string {
   882→	env.T.Helper()
   883→
   884→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   885→	if err != nil {
   886→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   887→	}
   888→
   889→	// Get the branch reference
   890→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
   891→	if err != nil {
   892→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get branch %s reference: %v", branchName, err)
   893→	}
   894→
   895→	// Get the commit
   896→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   897→	if err != nil {
   898→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get commit object: %v", err)
   899→	}
   900→
   901→	return commit.Message
   902→}
   903→
   904→// GitCommitWithShadowHooks stages and commits files, simulating the prepare-commit-msg
   905→// and post-commit hooks as a human (with TTY). This is the default for tests.
   906→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithShadowHooks(message string, files ...string) {
   907→	env.T.Helper()
   908→	env.gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message, true, files...)
   909→}
   910→
   911→// GitCommitWithShadowHooksAsAgent is like GitCommitWithShadowHooks but simulates
   912→// an agent commit (no TTY). This triggers the fast path in PrepareCommitMsg that
   913→// skips content detection and interactive prompts for ACTIVE sessions.
   914→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithShadowHooksAsAgent(message string, files ...string) {
   915→	env.T.Helper()
   916→	env.gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message, false, files...)
   917→}
   918→
   919→// gitCommitWithShadowHooks is the shared implementation for committing with shadow hooks.
   920→// When simulateTTY is true, sets ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 to simulate a human at the terminal.
   921→// When false, filters it out to simulate an agent subprocess (no controlling terminal).
   922→func (env *TestEnv) gitCommitWithShadowHooks(message string, simulateTTY bool, files ...string) {
   923→	env.T.Helper()
   924→
   925→	// Stage files using go-git
   926→	for _, file := range files {
   927→		env.GitAdd(file)
   928→	}
   929→
   930→	// Create a temp file for the commit message (prepare-commit-msg hook modifies this)
   931→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
   932→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
   933→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
   934→	}
   935→
   936→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook using the shared binary.
   937→	// Pass source="message" to match real `git commit -m` behavior.
   938→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile, "message")
   939→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   940→	if simulateTTY {
   941→		// Simulate human at terminal: ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 makes hasTTY() return true
   942→		// and askConfirmTTY() return defaultYes without reading from /dev/tty.
   943→		prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1")
   944→	} else {
   945→		// Simulate agent: ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0 makes hasTTY() return false,
   946→		// triggering the fast path that adds trailers for ACTIVE sessions.
   947→		prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0")
   948→	}
   949→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   950→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
   951→		// Don't fail - hook may silently succeed
   952→	}
   953→
   954→	// Read the modified message
   955→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
   956→	if err != nil {
   957→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
   958→	}
   959→
   960→	// Create the commit using go-git with the modified message
   961→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
   962→	if err != nil {
   963→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
   964→	}
   965→
   966→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   967→	if err != nil {
   968→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
   969→	}
   970→
   971→	_, err = worktree.Commit(string(modifiedMsg), &git.CommitOptions{
   972→		Author: &object.Signature{
   973→			Name:  "Test User",
   974→			Email: "test@example.com",
   975→			When:  time.Now(),
   976→		},
   977→	})
   978→	if err != nil {
   979→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
   980→	}
   981→
   982→	// Run post-commit hook using the shared binary
   983→	// This triggers condensation if the commit has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer
   984→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
   985→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   986→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   987→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
   988→		// Don't fail - hook may silently succeed
   989→	}
   990→}
   991→
   992→// GitCommitAmendWithShadowHooks amends the last commit with shadow hooks.
   993→// This simulates `git commit --amend` with the prepare-commit-msg and post-commit hooks.
   994→// The prepare-commit-msg hook is called with "commit" source to indicate an amend.
   995→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitAmendWithShadowHooks(message string, files ...string) {
   996→	env.T.Helper()
   997→
   998→	// Stage any additional files
   999→	for _, file := range files {
  1000→		env.GitAdd(file)
  1001→	}
  1002→
  1003→	// Write commit message to temp file
  1004→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
  1005→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
  1006→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
  1007→	}
  1008→
  1009→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook with "commit" source (indicates amend).
  1010→	// Set ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 to simulate human (amend is always a human operation).
  1011→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile, "commit")
  1012→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1013→	prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1")
  1014→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1015→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg (amend) output: %s", output)
  1016→	}
  1017→
  1018→	// Read the modified message
  1019→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
  1020→	if err != nil {
  1021→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
  1022→	}
  1023→
  1024→	// Amend the commit using go-git
  1025→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1026→	if err != nil {
  1027→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1028→	}
  1029→
  1030→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
  1031→	if err != nil {
  1032→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
  1033→	}
  1034→
  1035→	_, err = worktree.Commit(string(modifiedMsg), &git.CommitOptions{
  1036→		Author: &object.Signature{
  1037→			Name:  "Test User",
  1038→			Email: "test@example.com",
  1039→			When:  time.Now(),
  1040→		},
  1041→		Amend: true,
  1042→	})
  1043→	if err != nil {
  1044→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to amend commit: %v", err)
  1045→	}
  1046→
  1047→	// Run post-commit hook
  1048→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
  1049→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1050→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1051→		env.T.Logf("post-commit (amend) output: %s", output)
  1052→	}
  1053→}
  1054→
  1055→// GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved stages and commits files, simulating what happens when
  1056→// a user removes the Entire-Checkpoint trailer during commit message editing.
  1057→// This tests the opt-out behavior where removing the trailer skips condensation.
  1058→func (env *TestEnv) GitCommitWithTrailerRemoved(message string, files ...string) {
  1059→	env.T.Helper()
  1060→
  1061→	// Stage files using go-git
  1062→	for _, file := range files {
  1063→		env.GitAdd(file)
  1064→	}
  1065→
  1066→	// Create a temp file for the commit message (prepare-commit-msg hook modifies this)
  1067→	msgFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "COMMIT_EDITMSG")
  1068→	if err := os.WriteFile(msgFile, []byte(message), 0o644); err != nil {
  1069→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to write commit message file: %v", err)
  1070→	}
  1071→
  1072→	// Run prepare-commit-msg hook using the shared binary.
  1073→	// Set ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 to simulate human (this tests the editor flow where
  1074→	// the user removes the trailer before committing).
  1075→	prepCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "prepare-commit-msg", msgFile)
  1076→	prepCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1077→	prepCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1")
  1078→	if output, err := prepCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1079→		env.T.Logf("prepare-commit-msg output: %s", output)
  1080→	}
  1081→
  1082→	// Read the modified message (with trailer added by hook)
  1083→	modifiedMsg, err := os.ReadFile(msgFile)
  1084→	if err != nil {
  1085→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to read modified commit message: %v", err)
  1086→	}
  1087→
  1088→	// REMOVE the Entire-Checkpoint trailer (simulating user editing the message)
  1089→	lines := strings.Split(string(modifiedMsg), "\n")
  1090→	var cleanedLines []string
  1091→	for _, line := range lines {
  1092→		// Skip the trailer and the comments about it
  1093→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
  1094→			continue
  1095→		}
  1096→		if strings.Contains(line, "Remove the Entire-Checkpoint trailer") {
  1097→			continue
  1098→		}
  1099→		if strings.Contains(line, "trailer will be added to your next commit") {
  1100→			continue
  1101→		}
  1102→		cleanedLines = append(cleanedLines, line)
  1103→	}
  1104→	cleanedMsg := strings.TrimRight(strings.Join(cleanedLines, "\n"), "\n") + "\n"
  1105→
  1106→	// Create the commit using go-git with the cleaned message (no trailer)
  1107→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1108→	if err != nil {
  1109→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1110→	}
  1111→
  1112→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
  1113→	if err != nil {
  1114→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get worktree: %v", err)
  1115→	}
  1116→
  1117→	_, err = worktree.Commit(cleanedMsg, &git.CommitOptions{
  1118→		Author: &object.Signature{
  1119→			Name:  "Test User",
  1120→			Email: "test@example.com",
  1121→			When:  time.Now(),
  1122→		},
  1123→	})
  1124→	if err != nil {
  1125→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to commit: %v", err)
  1126→	}
  1127→
  1128→	// Run post-commit hook - since trailer was removed, no condensation should happen
  1129→	postCmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "git", "post-commit")
  1130→	postCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
  1131→	if output, err := postCmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1132→		env.T.Logf("post-commit output: %s", output)
  1133→	}
  1134→}
  1135→
  1136→// ListBranchesWithPrefix returns all branches that start with the given prefix.
  1137→func (env *TestEnv) ListBranchesWithPrefix(prefix string) []string {
  1138→	env.T.Helper()
  1139→
  1140→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1141→	if err != nil {
  1142→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1143→	}
  1144→
  1145→	refs, err := repo.References()
  1146→	if err != nil {
  1147→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get references: %v", err)
  1148→	}
  1149→
  1150→	var branches []string
  1151→	_ = refs.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
  1152→		name := ref.Name().Short()
  1153→		if len(name) >= len(prefix) && name[:len(prefix)] == prefix {
  1154→			branches = append(branches, name)
  1155→		}
  1156→		return nil
  1157→	})
  1158→
  1159→	return branches
  1160→}
  1161→
  1162→// GetLatestCheckpointID returns the most recent checkpoint ID from the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
  1163→// This is used by tests that previously extracted the checkpoint ID from commit message trailers.
  1164→// Now that active branch commits are clean (no trailers), we get the ID from the sessions branch.
  1165→// Fatals if the checkpoint ID cannot be found, with detailed context about what was found.
  1166→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointID() string {
  1167→	env.T.Helper()
  1168→
  1169→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1170→	if err != nil {
  1171→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1172→	}
  1173→
  1174→	// Get the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch
  1175→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
  1176→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
  1177→	if err != nil {
  1178→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get %s branch: %v", paths.MetadataBranchName, err)
  1179→	}
  1180→
  1181→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
  1182→	if err != nil {
  1183→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get commit: %v", err)
  1184→	}
  1185→
  1186→	// Extract checkpoint ID from commit message
  1187→	// Format: "Checkpoint: <12-hex-char-id>\n\nSession: ...\nStrategy: ..."
  1188→	for _, line := range strings.Split(commit.Message, "\n") {
  1189→		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
  1190→		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ") {
  1191→			return strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Checkpoint: ")
  1192→		}
  1193→	}
  1194→
  1195→	env.T.Fatalf("could not find checkpoint ID in %s branch commit message:\n%s",
  1196→		paths.MetadataBranchName, commit.Message)
  1197→	return ""
  1198→}

  1201→// Returns empty string if the branch doesn't exist or has no checkpoint commits yet.

  1203→func (env *TestEnv) TryGetLatestCheckpointID() string {

  1240→// GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage extracts the Entire-Checkpoint trailer from a commit message.
  1241→// Returns empty string if no trailer found.
  1242→func (env *TestEnv) GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage(commitSHA string) string {
  1243→	env.T.Helper()
  1244→
  1245→	msg := env.GetCommitMessage(commitSHA)
  1246→	cpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(msg)
  1247→	if !found {
  1248→		return ""
  1249→	}
  1250→	return cpID.String()
  1251→}
  1252→
  1253→// GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory walks backwards from HEAD on the active branch
  1254→// and returns the checkpoint ID from the first commit that has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer.
  1255→// This verifies that condensation actually happened (commit has trailer) without relying
  1256→// on timestamp-based matching.
  1257→func (env *TestEnv) GetLatestCheckpointIDFromHistory() string {
  1258→	env.T.Helper()
  1259→
  1260→	repo, err := git.PlainOpen(env.RepoDir)
  1261→	if err != nil {
  1262→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to open git repo: %v", err)
  1263→	}
  1264→
  1265→	head, err := repo.Head()
  1266→	if err != nil {
  1267→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to get HEAD: %v", err)
  1268→	}
  1269→
  1270→	commitIter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: head.Hash()})
  1271→	if err != nil {
  1272→		env.T.Fatalf("failed to iterate commits: %v", err)
  1273→	}
  1274→
  1275→	var checkpointID string
  1276→	//nolint:errcheck // ForEach callback handles errors
  1277→	commitIter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
  1278→		if cpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(c.Message); found {
  1279→			checkpointID = cpID.String()
  1280→			return errors.New("stop iteration") // Found it, stop
  1281→		}
  1282→		return nil
  1283→	})
  1284→
  1285→	if checkpointID == "" {
  1286→		env.T.Fatalf("no commit with Entire-Checkpoint trailer found in history")
  1287→	}
  1288→
  1289→	return checkpointID
  1290→}

  1292→// ShardedCheckpointPath returns the sharded path for a checkpoint ID.

  1295→func ShardedCheckpointPath(checkpointID string) string {

  1299→// SessionFilePath returns the path to a session file within a checkpoint.
  1300→// Session files are stored in numbered subdirectories using 0-based indexing (e.g., 0/full.jsonl).
  1301→// This function constructs the path for the first (default) session.
  1302→func SessionFilePath(checkpointID string, fileName string) string {
  1303→	return id.CheckpointID(checkpointID).Path() + "/0/" + fileName
  1304→}

  1306→// CheckpointSummaryPath returns the path to the root metadata.json (CheckpointSummary) for a checkpoint.
  1307→func CheckpointSummaryPath(checkpointID string) string {

  1309→}
  1310→
  1311→// SessionMetadataPath returns the path to the session-level metadata.json for a checkpoint.
  1312→func SessionMetadataPath(checkpointID string) string {

  1314→}

  1316→func findModuleRoot() string {
  1317→	// Start from this source file's location and walk up to find go.mod
  1318→	_, thisFile, _, ok := runtime.Caller(0)
  1319→	if !ok {
  1320→		panic("failed to get current file path via runtime.Caller")
  1321→	}
  1322→	dir := filepath.Dir(thisFile)
  1323→
  1324→	for {
  1325→		if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(dir, "go.mod")); err == nil {
  1326→			return dir
  1327→		}
  1328→		parent := filepath.Dir(dir)
  1329→		if parent == dir {
  1330→			panic("could not find go.mod starting from " + thisFile)
  1331→		}
  1332→		dir = parent
  1333→	}
  1334→}

    19→// TestShadow_FullWorkflow tests the complete shadow workflow as described in
    20→// docs/requirements/shadow-strategy/example.md
    21→//
    22→// This test simulates Alice's workflow:
    23→// 1. Start session, create checkpoints
    24→// 2. Rewind to earlier checkpoint
    25→// 3. Create new checkpoint after rewind
    26→// 4. User commits (triggers condensation)
    27→// 5. Continue working after commit (new shadow branch)
    28→// 6. User commits again (second condensation)
    29→// 7. Verify final state
    30→func TestShadow_FullWorkflow(t *testing.T) {
    31→	t.Parallel()
    32→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
    33→	defer env.Cleanup()

    44→
    45→	// Switch to feature branch (shadow skips main/master)
    46→	env.GitCheckoutNewBranch("feature/auth")
    47→

    49→	env.InitEntire(strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit)

    43→// TestGetGitAuthorFallbackToGitCommand tests that GetGitAuthor falls back
    44→// to the git command when go-git can't find user config in the repo's local config.
    45→// We set global config via environment variables, simulating a user who has
    46→// global git config but no local repo config.
    47→func TestGetGitAuthorFallbackToGitCommand(t *testing.T) {
    48→	t.Parallel()
    49→
    50→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
    51→
    52→	// Initialize repo using git command (not go-git) to avoid setting local config
    53→	cmd := exec.Command("git", "init")
    54→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
    55→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
    56→		t.Fatalf("git init failed: %v", err)
    57→	}
    58→
    59→	// Disable GPG signing for test commits
    60→	configCmd := exec.Command("git", "config", "commit.gpgsign", "false")
    61→	configCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
    62→	if err := configCmd.Run(); err != nil {
    63→		t.Fatalf("git config commit.gpgsign failed: %v", err)
    64→	}
    65→
    66→	// The repo now has no local user config. We'll use GIT_AUTHOR_* and GIT_COMMITTER_*
    67→	// env vars for commits, simulating global config that go-git can't see but git command can.
    68→
    69→	env.InitEntire("manual-commit")
    70→
    71→	// Create initial commit using environment variables for author/committer
    72→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test")
    73→
    74→	addCmd := exec.Command("git", "add", "README.md")
    75→	addCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
    76→	if err := addCmd.Run(); err != nil {
    77→		t.Fatalf("git add failed: %v", err)
    78→	}
    79→
    80→	// Use environment variables to set author and committer (works in CI without global config)
    81→	commitCmd := exec.Command("git", "commit", "-m", "Initial")
    82→	commitCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
    83→	commitCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
    84→		"GIT_AUTHOR_NAME=Test User",

    86→		"GIT_COMMITTER_NAME=Test User",

    88→	)

    95→	branchCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
    96→	if err := branchCmd.Run(); err != nil {
    97→		t.Fatalf("git checkout -b failed: %v", err)
    98→	}
    99→

   119→// TestGetGitAuthorNoConfigReturnsDefaults tests that when no config exists anywhere,
   120→// the function returns defaults instead of erroring.
   121→func TestGetGitAuthorNoConfigReturnsDefaults(t *testing.T) {
   122→	t.Parallel()
   123→
   124→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
   125→

   132→	initCmd.Env = []string{
   133→		"HOME=" + fakeHome,
   134→		"PATH=" + os.Getenv("PATH"),
   135→		"GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1", // Ignore system config
   136→	}
   137→	if err := initCmd.Run(); err != nil {
   138→		t.Fatalf("git init failed: %v", err)
   139→	}

   148→	}

   151→	}
   152→
   153→	env.InitEntire("manual-commit")

   156→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test")

   159→	addCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir

   219→// TestGetGitAuthorRemovingLocalConfig tests that removing the [user] section
   220→// from local config still works via fallback.
   221→func TestGetGitAuthorRemovingLocalConfig(t *testing.T) {
   222→	t.Parallel()
   223→
   224→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
   225→	env.InitRepo()
   226→
   227→	// Read and modify .git/config to remove user section
   228→	configPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "config")
   229→	data, err := os.ReadFile(configPath)
   230→	if err != nil {
   231→		t.Fatalf("failed to read .git/config: %v", err)
   232→	}
   233→
   234→	// Remove [user] section from config
   235→	configWithoutUser := removeUserSection(string(data))
   236→	if err := os.WriteFile(configPath, []byte(configWithoutUser), 0o644); err != nil {
   237→		t.Fatalf("failed to write .git/config: %v", err)
   238→	}
   239→
   240→	env.InitEntire("manual-commit")
   241→
   242→	// Need to create initial commit - use environment variables (works in CI without global config)
   243→	env.WriteFile("README.md", "# Test")
   244→
   245→	addCmd := exec.Command("git", "add", "README.md")
   246→	addCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   247→	addCmd.Run()
   248→
   249→	commitCmd := exec.Command("git", "commit", "-m", "Initial")
   250→	commitCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   251→	commitCmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   252→		"GIT_AUTHOR_NAME=Test User",
   253→		"GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL=test@example.com",
   254→		"GIT_COMMITTER_NAME=Test User",
   255→		"GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL=test@example.com",
   256→	)
   257→	commitCmd.Run()
   258→
   259→	// Create feature branch
   260→	branchCmd := exec.Command("git", "checkout", "-b", "feature/test")
   261→	branchCmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   262→	branchCmd.Run()
   263→
   264→	env.WriteFile("test.txt", "content")
   265→
   266→	// The CLI should still work because GetGitAuthor falls back
   267→	session := env.NewSession()
   268→	err = env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)
   269→	if err != nil {
   270→		errStr := err.Error()
   271→		if strings.Contains(errStr, "git user not configured") ||
   272→			strings.Contains(errStr, "git user.name not configured") ||
   273→			strings.Contains(errStr, "git user.email not configured") {
   274→			t.Errorf("GetGitAuthor should fall back when local config is removed.\nError: %s", errStr)
   275→		}
   276→		t.Logf("SimulateUserPromptSubmit output: %v", err)
   277→	}
   278→}

   280→// TestGetGitAuthorFromRepoReturnsDefaults verifies that GetGitAuthorFromRepo
   281→// returns "Unknown" and "unknown@local" when config is missing.
   282→func TestGetGitAuthorFromRepoReturnsDefaults(t *testing.T) {
   283→	t.Parallel()
   284→
   285→	// Create temp directory for test repo
   286→	tmpDir := t.TempDir()
   287→
   288→	// Initialize repo without setting user config
   289→	repo, err := git.PlainInit(tmpDir, false)
   290→	if err != nil {
   291→		t.Fatalf("Failed to init repo: %v", err)
   292→	}
   293→
   294→	// Import strategy package to test GetGitAuthorFromRepo directly
   295→	// Note: We can't import cli package from integration tests due to import cycles,
   296→	// but we can verify the behavior through the CLI commands above.
   297→
   298→	// Verify repo has no user config set
   299→	cfg, err := repo.Config()
   300→	if err != nil {
   301→		t.Fatalf("Failed to get repo config: %v", err)
   302→	}
   303→
   304→	if cfg.User.Name != "" || cfg.User.Email != "" {
   305→		t.Errorf("Expected empty user config, got Name=%q Email=%q", cfg.User.Name, cfg.User.Email)
   306→	}
   307→}
   308→
   309→// removeUserSection removes the [user] section from git config content.
   310→func removeUserSection(config string) string {
   311→	lines := strings.Split(config, "\n")
   312→	var result []string
   313→	inUserSection := false
   314→
   315→	for _, line := range lines {
   316→		trimmed := strings.TrimSpace(line)
   317→		if strings.HasPrefix(trimmed, "[user]") {
   318→			inUserSection = true
   319→			continue
   320→		}
   321→		if inUserSection && strings.HasPrefix(trimmed, "[") {
   322→			inUserSection = false
   323→		}
   324→		if !inUserSection {
   325→			result = append(result, line)
   326→		}
   327→	}
   328→
   329→	return strings.Join(result, "\n")
   330→}

    17→// HookRunner executes CLI hooks in the test environment.
    18→type HookRunner struct {
    19→	RepoDir          string
    20→	ClaudeProjectDir string
    21→	T                interface {
    22→		Helper()
    23→		Fatalf(format string, args ...interface{})
    24→		Logf(format string, args ...interface{})
    25→	}
    26→}
    27→
    28→// NewHookRunner creates a new hook runner for the given repo directory.
    29→func NewHookRunner(repoDir, claudeProjectDir string, t interface {
    30→	Helper()
    31→	Fatalf(format string, args ...interface{})
    32→	Logf(format string, args ...interface{})
    33→}) *HookRunner {
    34→	return &HookRunner{
    35→		RepoDir:          repoDir,
    36→		ClaudeProjectDir: claudeProjectDir,
    37→		T:                t,
    38→	}
    39→}
    40→
    41→// HookResponse represents the JSON response from Claude Code hooks.
    42→type HookResponse struct {
    43→	Continue   bool   `json:"continue"`
    44→	StopReason string `json:"stopReason,omitempty"`
    45→}
    46→
    47→// SimulateUserPromptSubmit simulates the UserPromptSubmit hook.
    48→// This captures pre-prompt state (untracked files).
    49→func (r *HookRunner) SimulateUserPromptSubmit(sessionID string) error {
    50→	r.T.Helper()
    51→
    52→	input := map[string]string{
    53→		"session_id":      sessionID,
    54→		"transcript_path": "", // Not used for user-prompt-submit
    55→	}
    56→
    57→	return r.runHookWithInput("user-prompt-submit", input)
    58→}
    59→
    60→// SimulateUserPromptSubmitWithResponse simulates the UserPromptSubmit hook
    61→// and returns the parsed hook response (for testing blocking behavior).
    62→func (r *HookRunner) SimulateUserPromptSubmitWithResponse(sessionID string) (*HookResponse, error) {
    63→	r.T.Helper()
    64→
    65→	input := map[string]string{
    66→		"session_id":      sessionID,
    67→		"transcript_path": "", // Not used for user-prompt-submit
    68→	}
    69→
    70→	inputJSON, err := json.Marshal(input)
    71→	if err != nil {
    72→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal hook input: %w", err)
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	output := r.runHookWithOutput("user-prompt-submit", inputJSON)
    76→
    77→	// If hook failed with an error, return the error
    78→	if output.Err != nil {
    79→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("hook failed: %w\nStderr: %s\nStdout: %s",
    80→			output.Err, output.Stderr, output.Stdout)
    81→	}
    82→
    83→	// Parse JSON response from stdout
    84→	var resp HookResponse
    85→	if len(output.Stdout) > 0 {
    86→		if err := json.Unmarshal(output.Stdout, &resp); err != nil {
    87→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse hook response: %w\nStdout: %s",
    88→				err, output.Stdout)
    89→		}
    90→	}
    91→
    92→	return &resp, nil
    93→}
    94→

    97→	r.T.Helper()
    98→
    99→	input := map[string]string{
   100→		"session_id":      sessionID,
   101→		"transcript_path": transcriptPath,
   102→	}
   103→
   104→	return r.runHookWithInput("stop", input)

   107→// PreTaskInput contains the input for PreToolUse[Task] hook.
   108→type PreTaskInput struct {
   109→	SessionID      string
   110→	TranscriptPath string
   111→	ToolUseID      string
   112→	SubagentType   string // Optional: type of subagent (e.g., "dev", "reviewer")
   113→	Description    string // Optional: task description
   114→}
   115→
   116→// SimulatePreTask simulates the PreToolUse[Task] hook.
   117→func (r *HookRunner) SimulatePreTask(sessionID, transcriptPath, toolUseID string) error {
   118→	r.T.Helper()
   119→
   120→	return r.SimulatePreTaskWithInput(PreTaskInput{
   121→		SessionID:      sessionID,
   122→		TranscriptPath: transcriptPath,
   123→		ToolUseID:      toolUseID,
   124→	})
   125→}
   126→
   127→// SimulatePreTaskWithInput simulates the PreToolUse[Task] hook with full input.
   128→func (r *HookRunner) SimulatePreTaskWithInput(input PreTaskInput) error {
   129→	r.T.Helper()
   130→
   131→	hookInput := map[string]interface{}{
   132→		"session_id":      input.SessionID,
   133→		"transcript_path": input.TranscriptPath,
   134→		"tool_use_id":     input.ToolUseID,
   135→		"tool_input": map[string]string{
   136→			"subagent_type": input.SubagentType,
   137→			"description":   input.Description,
   138→		},
   139→	}
   140→
   141→	return r.runHookWithInput("pre-task", hookInput)
   142→}
   143→
   144→// PostTaskInput contains the input for PostToolUse[Task] hook.
   145→type PostTaskInput struct {
   146→	SessionID      string
   147→	TranscriptPath string
   148→	ToolUseID      string
   149→	AgentID        string
   150→}
   151→
   152→// SimulatePostTask simulates the PostToolUse[Task] hook.
   153→func (r *HookRunner) SimulatePostTask(input PostTaskInput) error {
   154→	r.T.Helper()
   155→
   156→	hookInput := map[string]interface{}{
   157→		"session_id":      input.SessionID,
   158→		"transcript_path": input.TranscriptPath,
   159→		"tool_use_id":     input.ToolUseID,
   160→		"tool_input":      map[string]string{},
   161→		"tool_response": map[string]string{
   162→			"agentId": input.AgentID,
   163→		},
   164→	}
   165→
   166→	return r.runHookWithInput("post-task", hookInput)
   167→}
   168→
   169→func (r *HookRunner) runHookWithInput(flag string, input interface{}) error {
   170→	r.T.Helper()
   171→
   172→	inputJSON, err := json.Marshal(input)
   173→	if err != nil {
   174→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal hook input: %w", err)
   175→	}
   176→
   177→	return r.runHookInRepoDir(flag, inputJSON)
   178→}
   179→
   180→func (r *HookRunner) runHookInRepoDir(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) error {
   181→	// Run using the shared test binary
   182→	// Command structure: entire hooks claude-code <hook-name>
   183→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "claude-code", hookName)
   184→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   185→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   186→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   187→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+r.ClaudeProjectDir,
   188→	)
   189→
   190→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   191→	if err != nil {
   192→		return fmt.Errorf("hook %s failed: %w\nInput: %s\nOutput: %s",
   193→			hookName, err, inputJSON, output)
   194→	}
   195→
   196→	r.T.Logf("Hook %s output: %s", hookName, output)
   197→	return nil
   198→}
   199→
   200→// Session represents a simulated Claude Code session.
   201→type Session struct {
   202→	ID                string // Raw model session ID (e.g., "test-session-1")
   203→	EntireID          string // Entire session ID with date prefix (e.g., "2025-12-02-test-session-1")
   204→	TranscriptPath    string
   205→	TranscriptBuilder *TranscriptBuilder
   206→	env               *TestEnv
   207→}
   208→
   209→// FileChange represents a file modification in a session.
   210→type FileChange struct {
   211→	Path    string
   212→	Content string
   213→}
   214→
   215→// NewSession creates a new simulated session.
   216→func (env *TestEnv) NewSession() *Session {
   217→	env.T.Helper()
   218→
   219→	env.SessionCounter++
   220→	sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("test-session-%d", env.SessionCounter)
   221→	entireID := sessionid.EntireSessionID(sessionID)
   222→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire", "tmp", sessionID+".jsonl")
   223→
   224→	return &Session{
   225→		ID:                sessionID,
   226→		EntireID:          entireID,
   227→		TranscriptPath:    transcriptPath,
   228→		TranscriptBuilder: NewTranscriptBuilder(),
   229→		env:               env,
   230→	}
   231→}
   232→
   233→// CreateTranscript builds and writes a transcript for the session.
   234→func (s *Session) CreateTranscript(prompt string, changes []FileChange) string {
   235→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddUserMessage(prompt)
   236→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddAssistantMessage("I'll help you with that.")
   237→
   238→	for _, change := range changes {
   239→		toolID := s.TranscriptBuilder.AddToolUse("mcp__acp__Write", change.Path, change.Content)
   240→		s.TranscriptBuilder.AddToolResult(toolID)
   241→	}
   242→
   243→	s.TranscriptBuilder.AddAssistantMessage("Done!")
   244→
   245→	if err := s.TranscriptBuilder.WriteToFile(s.TranscriptPath); err != nil {
   246→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to write transcript: %v", err)
   247→	}
   248→
   249→	return s.TranscriptPath
   250→}

   327→// ClearSessionState removes the session state file for the given session ID.
   328→// This simulates what happens when a user commits their changes (session is "completed").
   329→// Used in tests to allow sequential sessions to run without triggering concurrent session warnings.
   330→func (env *TestEnv) ClearSessionState(sessionID string) error {
   331→	env.T.Helper()
   332→
   333→	// Session state is stored in .git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json
   334→	// Use sessionid.EntireSessionID to get the full session ID with date prefix
   335→	entireSessionID := sessionid.EntireSessionID(sessionID)
   336→	stateFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "entire-sessions", entireSessionID+".json")
   337→
   338→	if err := os.Remove(stateFile); err != nil && !os.IsNotExist(err) {
   339→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to clear session state: %w", err)
   340→	}
   341→	return nil
   342→}

   344→// HookOutput contains the result of running a hook.
   345→type HookOutput struct {
   346→	Stdout []byte
   347→	Stderr []byte
   348→	Err    error
   349→}
   350→
   351→// runHookWithOutput runs a hook and returns both stdout and stderr separately.
   352→func (r *HookRunner) runHookWithOutput(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) HookOutput {
   353→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "claude-code", hookName)
   354→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   355→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   356→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   357→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+r.ClaudeProjectDir,
   358→	)
   359→
   360→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   361→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   362→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   363→
   364→	err := cmd.Run()
   365→	return HookOutput{
   366→		Stdout: stdout.Bytes(),
   367→		Stderr: stderr.Bytes(),
   368→		Err:    err,
   369→	}
   370→}

   420→// GetSessionState reads and returns the session state for the given session ID.
   421→func (env *TestEnv) GetSessionState(sessionID string) (*strategy.SessionState, error) {
   422→	env.T.Helper()
   423→
   424→	entireSessionID := sessionid.EntireSessionID(sessionID)
   425→	stateFile := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git", "entire-sessions", entireSessionID+".json")
   426→
   427→	data, err := os.ReadFile(stateFile)
   428→	if os.IsNotExist(err) {
   429→		return nil, nil
   430→	}
   431→	if err != nil {
   432→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read session state: %w", err)
   433→	}
   434→
   435→	var state strategy.SessionState
   436→	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &state); err != nil {
   437→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse session state: %w", err)
   438→	}
   439→	return &state, nil
   440→}

   442→// GeminiHookRunner executes Gemini CLI hooks in the test environment.
   443→type GeminiHookRunner struct {
   444→	RepoDir          string
   445→	GeminiProjectDir string
   446→	T                interface {
   447→		Helper()
   448→		Fatalf(format string, args ...interface{})
   449→		Logf(format string, args ...interface{})
   450→	}
   451→}
   452→
   453→// NewGeminiHookRunner creates a new Gemini hook runner for the given repo directory.
   454→func NewGeminiHookRunner(repoDir, geminiProjectDir string, t interface {
   455→	Helper()
   456→	Fatalf(format string, args ...interface{})
   457→	Logf(format string, args ...interface{})
   458→}) *GeminiHookRunner {
   459→	return &GeminiHookRunner{
   460→		RepoDir:          repoDir,
   461→		GeminiProjectDir: geminiProjectDir,
   462→		T:                t,
   463→	}
   464→}
   465→
   466→// runGeminiHookWithInput runs a Gemini hook with the given input.
   467→func (r *GeminiHookRunner) runGeminiHookWithInput(hookName string, input interface{}) error {
   468→	r.T.Helper()
   469→
   470→	inputJSON, err := json.Marshal(input)
   471→	if err != nil {
   472→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal hook input: %w", err)
   473→	}
   474→
   475→	return r.runGeminiHookInRepoDir(hookName, inputJSON)
   476→}
   477→
   478→func (r *GeminiHookRunner) runGeminiHookInRepoDir(hookName string, inputJSON []byte) error {
   479→	// Run using the shared test binary
   480→	// Command structure: entire hooks gemini <hook-name>
   481→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "gemini", hookName)
   482→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir
   483→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   484→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   485→		"ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR="+r.GeminiProjectDir,
   486→	)
   487→
   488→	output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   489→	if err != nil {
   490→		return fmt.Errorf("hook %s failed: %w\nInput: %s\nOutput: %s",
   491→			hookName, err, inputJSON, output)
   492→	}
   493→
   494→	r.T.Logf("Gemini hook %s output: %s", hookName, output)
   495→	return nil
   496→}
   497→

   501→	cmd.Dir = r.RepoDir

   503→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),

   506→
   507→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer

   509→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   510→
   511→	err := cmd.Run()
   512→	return HookOutput{

   515→		Err:    err,
   516→	}
   517→}

   590→// GeminiSession represents a simulated Gemini CLI session.
   591→type GeminiSession struct {
   592→	ID             string // Raw model session ID (e.g., "gemini-session-1")
   593→	EntireID       string // Entire session ID with date prefix (e.g., "2025-12-02-gemini-session-1")
   594→	TranscriptPath string
   595→	env            *TestEnv
   596→}
   597→
   598→// NewGeminiSession creates a new simulated Gemini session.
   599→func (env *TestEnv) NewGeminiSession() *GeminiSession {
   600→	env.T.Helper()
   601→
   602→	env.SessionCounter++
   603→	sessionID := fmt.Sprintf("gemini-session-%d", env.SessionCounter)
   604→	entireID := sessionid.EntireSessionID(sessionID)
   605→	transcriptPath := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire", "tmp", sessionID+".json")
   606→
   607→	return &GeminiSession{
   608→		ID:             sessionID,
   609→		EntireID:       entireID,
   610→		TranscriptPath: transcriptPath,
   611→		env:            env,
   612→	}
   613→}
   614→
   615→// CreateGeminiTranscript creates a Gemini JSON transcript file for the session.
   616→func (s *GeminiSession) CreateGeminiTranscript(prompt string, changes []FileChange) string {
   617→	// Build Gemini-format transcript (JSON, not JSONL)
   618→	messages := []map[string]interface{}{
   619→		{
   620→			"type":    "user",
   621→			"content": prompt,
   622→		},
   623→		{
   624→			"type":    "assistant",
   625→			"content": "I'll help you with that.",
   626→		},
   627→	}
   628→
   629→	for _, change := range changes {
   630→		messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   631→			"type": "tool_use",
   632→			"name": "write_file",
   633→			"input": map[string]string{
   634→				"path":    change.Path,
   635→				"content": change.Content,
   636→			},
   637→		})
   638→		messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   639→			"type":   "tool_result",
   640→			"output": "File written successfully",
   641→		})
   642→	}
   643→
   644→	messages = append(messages, map[string]interface{}{
   645→		"type":    "assistant",
   646→		"content": "Done!",
   647→	})
   648→
   649→	transcript := map[string]interface{}{
   650→		"sessionId": s.ID,
   651→		"messages":  messages,
   652→	}
   653→
   654→	// Ensure directory exists
   655→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(s.TranscriptPath), 0o755); err != nil {
   656→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to create transcript dir: %v", err)
   657→	}
   658→
   659→	// Write transcript
   660→	data, err := json.MarshalIndent(transcript, "", "  ")
   661→	if err != nil {
   662→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to marshal transcript: %v", err)
   663→	}
   664→	if err := os.WriteFile(s.TranscriptPath, data, 0o644); err != nil {
   665→		s.env.T.Fatalf("failed to write transcript: %v", err)
   666→	}
   667→
   668→	return s.TranscriptPath
   669→}
   670→

    15→func TestNewTestEnv(t *testing.T) {
    16→	t.Parallel()
    17→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
    18→
    19→	// Verify RepoDir exists
    20→	if _, err := os.Stat(env.RepoDir); os.IsNotExist(err) {
    21→		t.Error("RepoDir should exist")
    22→	}
    23→
    24→	// Verify ClaudeProjectDir exists
    25→	if _, err := os.Stat(env.ClaudeProjectDir); os.IsNotExist(err) {
    26→		t.Error("ClaudeProjectDir should exist")
    27→	}
    28→
    29→	// Verify ClaudeProjectDir is set in struct (no longer uses env var for parallel test compatibility)
    30→	if env.ClaudeProjectDir == "" {
    31→		t.Error("ClaudeProjectDir should not be empty")
    32→	}
    33→
    34→	// Note: NewTestEnv no longer changes working directory or uses t.Setenv
    35→	// to allow parallel execution. CLI commands receive env vars via cmd.Env.
    36→}
    37→
    38→func TestTestEnv_InitRepo(t *testing.T) {
    39→	t.Parallel()
    40→	env := NewTestEnv(t)
    41→	env.InitRepo()
    42→
    43→	// Verify .git directory exists
    44→	gitDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".git")
    45→	if _, err := os.Stat(gitDir); os.IsNotExist(err) {
    46→		t.Error(".git directory should exist after InitRepo")
    47→	}
    48→}
    49→
    50→func TestTestEnv_InitEntire(t *testing.T) {
    51→	t.Parallel()
    52→	RunForAllStrategiesWithBasicEnv(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {
    53→		// Verify .entire directory exists
    54→		entireDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, ".entire")
    55→		if _, err := os.Stat(entireDir); os.IsNotExist(err) {
    56→			t.Error(".entire directory should exist")

Now I have a clear understanding of the patterns. The test needs to:

1. Create a session, make changes, create a checkpoint
2. Trigger condensation (commit with hooks)
3. Read the `CheckpointSummary` from `entire/checkpoints/v1` and verify `cli_version` is set
4. Read the session state file and verify `cli_version` is set

Let me also check what `buildinfo.Version` defaults to in tests (it should be `"dev"` since tests don't use ldflags).

Now let me write the test.

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01CXXkaGEHxLQt5n68nhpkAx
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/gtrrz-victor/wks/test-cli/cli/cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/cli_version_metadata_test.go",
  "content": "//go:build integration\n\npackage integration\n\nimport (\n\t\"encoding/json\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\n\t\"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint\"\n\t\"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/paths\"\n)\n\n// TestCLIVersionInMetadata verifies that cli_version is present in both\n// the CheckpointSummary (on entire/checkpoints/v1) and the session state\n// file (in .git/entire-sessions/).\nfunc TestCLIVersionInMetadata(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\tRunForAllStrategies(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\t// 1. Start a session and create a checkpoint\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\tif err := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID); err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Verify cli_version is set in the session state file\n\t\tstate, err := env.GetSessionState(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"GetSessionState failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif state == nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"session state should exist\")\n\t\t}\n\t\tif state.CLIVersion == \"\" {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"session state cli_version should not be empty\")\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tt.Logf(\"session state cli_version = %q\", state.CLIVersion)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// 2. Create a file change and checkpoint\n\t\tenv.WriteFile(\"version-test.txt\", \"hello\")\n\t\tsession.CreateTranscript(\n\t\t\t\"Create a test file\",\n\t\t\t[]FileChange{{Path: \"version-test.txt\", Content: \"hello\"}},\n\t\t)\n\t\tif err := env.SimulateStop(session.ID, session.TranscriptPath); err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateStop failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// 3. Trigger condensation by committing with hooks\n\t\tenv.WriteFile(\"version-test.txt\", \"hello v2\")\n\t\tenv.GitCommitWithShadowHooks(\"Add version test file\", \"version-test.txt\")\n\n\t\t// 4. Read CheckpointSummary from entire/checkpoints/v1 branch\n\t\tif !env.BranchExists(paths.MetadataBranchName) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"entire/checkpoints/v1 branch should exist after condensation\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tcheckpointID := env.GetLatestCheckpointID()\n\t\tsummaryPath := CheckpointSummaryPath(checkpointID)\n\t\tsummaryContent, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(paths.MetadataBranchName, summaryPath)\n\t\tif !found {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"root metadata.json should be readable\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tvar summary checkpoint.CheckpointSummary\n\t\tif err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(summaryContent), &summary); err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"failed to parse CheckpointSummary: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif summary.CLIVersion == \"\" {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"CheckpointSummary cli_version should not be empty\")\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tt.Logf(\"CheckpointSummary cli_version = %q\", summary.CLIVersion)\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}\n"
}

Both strategies pass. The test verifies:

1. **Session state** (`cli_version = "dev"`) — set during `initializeSession` / `InitializeSession`
2. **CheckpointSummary** (`cli_version = "dev"`) — set during `writeCheckpointSummary` in committed.go

The value is `"dev"` because the test binary is built without ldflags, which is the correct default.

    14→func TestMain(m *testing.M) {

    17→	if err != nil {
    18→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "failed to create temp dir for binary: %v\n", err)

    23→

    26→	buildCmd.Dir = filepath.Join(moduleRoot, "cmd", "entire")

    31→		os.RemoveAll(tmpDir)
    32→		os.Exit(1)
    33→	}
    34→
    35→	// Run tests
    36→	code := m.Run()
    37→
    38→	// Cleanup
    39→	os.RemoveAll(tmpDir)
    40→	os.Exit(code)
    41→}

Excellent! Now I have comprehensive information. Let me compile a thorough report:

## Summary of Test Patterns in Checkpoint and Strategy Packages

Based on my thorough analysis of the codebase, here's what I found:

### 1. **Checkpoint Package Tests** (`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/`)

**Test Files:**
- `checkpoint_test.go` - Main test file with 40+ test functions
- `backwards_compat_test.go` - Backward compatibility tests
- `temporary_test.go` - Shadow branch tests
- `id/id_test.go` - Checkpoint ID tests

**Key Test Pattern for `WriteCommitted`:**

Test function signature (lines 101-218 from `TestWriteCommitted_AgentField`):

```go
func TestWriteCommitted_AgentField(t *testing.T) {
    tempDir := t.TempDir()
    repo, err := git.PlainInit(tempDir, false)
    // ... setup git repo with initial commit
    
    store := NewGitStore(repo)
    checkpointID := id.MustCheckpointID("a1b2c3d4e5f6")
    
    // Call WriteCommitted with options
    err = store.WriteCommitted(context.Background(), WriteCommittedOptions{
        CheckpointID: checkpointID,
        SessionID:    sessionID,
        Strategy:     "manual-commit",
        Agent:        agentType,
        Transcript:   []byte("test transcript content"),
        AuthorName:   "Test Author",
        AuthorEmail:  "test@example.com",
    })
    
    // Verify by reading from metadata branch
    ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName), true)
    commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
    tree, err := commit.Tree()
    checkpointTree, err := tree.Tree(checkpointID.Path())  // Sharded path
    metadataFile, err := checkpointTree.File(paths.MetadataFileName)
    content, err := metadataFile.Contents()
    
    // Parse and verify JSON
    var summary CheckpointSummary
    json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &summary)
    // Assert on summary fields
}
```

**Related Test Pattern - `TestWriteCommitted_Aggregation`** (lines 937-1024):

Writes multiple sessions to same checkpoint and verifies aggregation:
- Creates two sessions with overlapping/new files
- Calls `store.ReadCommitted()` to read back
- Asserts on `CheckpointsCount`, `FilesTouched` (merged/deduplicated), and `TokenUsage` (summed)

**Key Helper Function - `readLatestSessionMetadata`** (lines 220-285):

Shows pattern for reading session-specific metadata from numbered subdirectories (0-based indexing):
```go

    // Navigate: metadata branch → checkpoint tree (sharded) → root metadata.json

}
```

```go
func TestReadCommitted_ReturnsCheckpointSummary(t *testing.T) {
    // Write multiple sessions
    for i, sessionID := range []string{"session-alpha", "session-beta"} {
        err := store.WriteCommitted(context.Background(), WriteCommittedOptions{
            CheckpointID:     checkpointID,
            SessionID:        sessionID,
            FilesTouched:     []string{fmt.Sprintf("file%d.go", i)},
            CheckpointsCount: i + 1,
            // ... other fields
        })
    }
    
    // Read and assert
    summary, err := store.ReadCommitted(context.Background(), checkpointID)
    
    // Verify Sessions array structure
    if len(summary.Sessions) != 2 { t.Fatalf(...) }
    for i, session := range summary.Sessions {
        expectedSubdir := fmt.Sprintf("/%d/", i)
        if !strings.Contains(session.Metadata, expectedSubdir) { ... }
    }
}
```

**Test Pattern - `TestReadSessionContent_ByIndex`** (lines 1089-1146):

Shows how to read individual session content by index:
```go
content0, err := store.ReadSessionContent(context.Background(), checkpointID, 0)
if content0.Metadata.SessionID != "session-first" { t.Errorf(...) }
if !strings.Contains(string(content0.Transcript), "first") { ... }
```

    Summary: &Summary{

})

### 2. **Strategy Package Tests** (`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/`)

**Test Files:**
- `phase_wiring_test.go` - InitializeSession tests with phase transitions
- `auto_commit_test.go` - AutoCommit strategy tests
- `manual_commit_test.go` - Manual-commit strategy tests
- `session_state_test.go` - Session state persistence tests

**Key Test Pattern - `TestAutoCommitStrategy_InitializeSession_CreatesSessionState`** (lines 668-725 from `auto_commit_test.go`):

```go
func TestAutoCommitStrategy_InitializeSession_CreatesSessionState(t *testing.T) {
    dir := t.TempDir()
    repo, err := git.PlainInit(dir, false)
    
    // Create initial commit (prerequisite)
    worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
    readmeFile := filepath.Join(dir, "README.md")
    os.WriteFile(readmeFile, []byte("# Test"), 0o644)
    worktree.Add("README.md")
    worktree.Commit("Initial commit", &git.CommitOptions{
        Author: &object.Signature{Name: "Test", Email: "test@test.com"},
    })
    
    t.Chdir(dir)
    
    s := NewAutoCommitStrategy()
    initializer, ok := s.(SessionInitializer)
    
    sessionID := "2025-12-22-test-session-init"
    err := initializer.InitializeSession(sessionID, "Claude Code", "", "")
    
    // Verify session state was created by reading JSON
    state, err := LoadSessionState(sessionID)
    
    // Assert on fields
    if state.SessionID != sessionID { t.Errorf(...) }
    if state.StepCount != 0 { t.Errorf(...) }
    if state.CheckpointTranscriptStart != 0 { t.Errorf(...) }
}
```

**Key Test Pattern - InitializeSession with Phase Transitions** (lines 18-35 from `phase_wiring_test.go`):

```go
func TestInitializeSession_SetsPhaseActive(t *testing.T) {
    dir := setupGitRepo(t)
    t.Chdir(dir)
    
    s := &ManualCommitStrategy{}
    
    err := s.InitializeSession("test-session-phase-1", "Claude Code", "", "")
    require.NoError(t, err)
    
    state, err := s.loadSessionState("test-session-phase-1")
    require.NoError(t, err)
    require.NotNil(t, state)
    
    // Assert on phase field
    assert.Equal(t, session.PhaseActive, state.Phase,
        "InitializeSession should set phase to ACTIVE")
    require.NotNil(t, state.LastInteractionTime,
        "InitializeSession should set LastInteractionTime")
}
```

**Session State Persistence Pattern** (lines 11-64 from `session_state_test.go`):

```go
func TestLoadSessionState_PackageLevel(t *testing.T) {
    dir := t.TempDir()
    git.PlainInit(dir, false)
    t.Chdir(dir)
    
    // Create session state struct
    state := &SessionState{
        SessionID:                 "test-session-pkg-123",
        BaseCommit:                "abc123def456",
        StartedAt:                 time.Now(),
        StepCount:                 3,
        CheckpointTranscriptStart: 150,
    }
    
    // Save to JSON (`.git/entire-sessions/`)
    err := SaveSessionState(state)
    
    // Load from JSON
    loaded, err := LoadSessionState("test-session-pkg-123")
    
    // Verify all fields
    verifySessionState(t, loaded, state)  // Helper function
}
```

**Verify Helper Function Pattern** (lines 49-64 from `session_state_test.go`):

```go
func verifySessionState(t *testing.T, loaded, expected *SessionState) {
    t.Helper()
    if loaded.SessionID != expected.SessionID {
        t.Errorf("SessionID = %q, want %q", loaded.SessionID, expected.SessionID)
    }
    if loaded.BaseCommit != expected.BaseCommit { ... }
    if loaded.StepCount != expected.StepCount { ... }
    if loaded.CheckpointTranscriptStart != expected.CheckpointTranscriptStart { ... }
}
```

**Backfill Pattern** (lines 1768-1805 from `manual_commit_test.go`):

Shows how tests verify field backfilling/repair:
```go
func TestInitializeSession_BackfillsUnknownAgentType(t *testing.T) {
    // Initialize session first
    s.InitializeSession(sessionID, agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode, "", "")
    
    // Simulate corrupted state (manually set to Unknown)
    state, err := s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
    state.AgentType = agent.AgentTypeUnknown
    s.saveSessionState(state)
    
    // Second call should repair it
    s.InitializeSession(sessionID, agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode, "", "")
    
    loaded, err := s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
    if loaded.AgentType != agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode {
        t.Errorf("AgentType = %q, want %q (should have been backfilled)",
            loaded.AgentType, agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode, agent.AgentTypeUnknown)
    }
}
```

### 3. **CLIVersion Field - Existing Usage (No Tests Yet)**

**Where CLIVersion is SET:**

1. **`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/committed.go` (line 350):**
   ```go
   sessionMetadata := &CommittedMetadata{
       // ...

   }
   ```

       CheckpointID: opts.CheckpointID,

       // ...
   }
   ```

3. **`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_session.go` (line 222):**
   ```go

       CLIVersion:  buildinfo.Version,  // <-- SET HERE

   }
   ```

4. **`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/auto_commit.go` (line 948):**

       SessionID:   sessionID,
       CLIVersion:  buildinfo.Version,  // <-- SET HERE
       BaseCommit:  baseCommit,
       // ...
   }
   ```

**Where CLIVersion is DEFINED:**

- **`cmd/entire/cli/buildinfo/buildinfo.go` (lines 4-6):**
  ```go
  var (
      Version = "dev"
      Commit  = "unknown"
  )
  ```

**Where CLIVersion is Used (but not yet tested):**

- Struct fields in:
  - `SessionState` (`cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go:31`)
  - `CommittedMetadata` (`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/checkpoint.go:331`)
  - `CheckpointSummary` (`cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/checkpoint.go:405`)
  - Version command output (`cmd/entire/cli/root.go:98`)
  - Telemetry tracking (`cmd/entire/cli/root.go:59`)

**NO existing tests verify CLIVersion is:**
- Actually set when calling `WriteCommitted()`
- Serialized correctly to JSON
- Deserialized correctly from JSON
- Equals `buildinfo.Version`
- Included in commit metadata on `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch

---

### **Recommended Test Structure for CLIVersion**

Based on the patterns above, a CLIVersion test should:

1. **Setup:** Create a git repo with initial commit, instantiate GitStore or Strategy
2. **Act:** Call `WriteCommitted()` or `InitializeSession()` with specific fields
3. **Verify:** 
   - Read JSON from git (either `entire/checkpoints/v1` or `.git/entire-sessions/`)
   - Parse as struct (CheckpointSummary, CommittedMetadata, SessionState)
   - Assert `CLIVersion == buildinfo.Version`
   - For checkpoint metadata, verify it's in commit trailer if applicable

**Example test locations based on existing patterns:**
- `cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/checkpoint_test.go` - Add test alongside `TestWriteCommitted_AgentField`
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/session_state_test.go` - Add test alongside other SessionState persistence tests
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/phase_wiring_test.go` - Add test alongside `TestInitializeSession_*` variants
